(65)

Then the 8–dimensional vector,
\mathbf{Z}
, given in eq. (
II.3
) can be treated as a phase space vector and the evolution equations (
32
) are indeed matrix Hamiltonian equations.

The function in eq. (
65
) can be viewed as a perturbation Hamiltonian to the standard Hamiltonian of a null geodesic
[
71
]
. Accordingly, there exists an associated symplectic form which is preserved throughout the Hamiltonian flow,

\displaystyle d\left({\Delta l}^{\mathscr{E}}_{{\tilde{b}}}\right)\wedge d\left({\delta x}_{\mathscr{E}}^{{\tilde{b}}}\right)=d\left({\Delta l}^{\mathscr{O}}_{{\tilde{b}}}\right)\wedge d\left({\delta x}_{\mathscr{O}}^{{\tilde{b}}}\right).

(66)

When compared to the symplectic structure in eq. (
27
), it is clear that the one in eq. (
66
) is representing the symplectic structure of the perturbation of the system, i.e., the deviation of geodesics.
Then due to the Poincaré’s lemma, there exists a generating function
S
where

\displaystyle{\Delta l}^{\mathscr{E}}_{{\tilde{b}}}d\left({\delta x}_{\mathscr{E}}^{{\tilde{b}}}\right)={\Delta l}^{\mathscr{O}}_{{\tilde{b}}}d\left({\delta x}_{\mathscr{O}}^{{\tilde{b}}}\right)+dS\left({\delta x}_{\mathscr{E}}^{{\tilde{a}}},{\Delta l}^{\mathscr{E}}_{{\tilde{b}}};\lambda\right).

The generating function in question is the action
S\left(\delta{x}^{\tilde{a}},\Delta{l}_{\tilde{b}}\right)
in eq. (
64
) and the symplectic matrix
\mathbf{W}
represents the associated linear canonical transformation.
The matrix
\mathbf{W}
provides a solution to the initial value problem of the position and tangent vector perturbations. Namely, given the initial values
{\delta x}_{\mathscr{O}}^{{\tilde{b}}}
and
{\Delta l}^{\mathscr{O}}_{{\tilde{b}}}
, one can find the final values
{\delta x}_{\mathscr{E}}^{{\tilde{b}}}
and
{\Delta l}^{\mathscr{E}}_{{\tilde{b}}}
through
\mathbf{W}
. In this work, we assume those to be
free canonical transformations

[
72
]
which are represented by
free symplectic matrices

[
73
]
. Such symplectic matrices are assumed to have a non–zero determinant for the upper right block, i.e.,

\displaystyle{\det}\mathchoice{\mathbf{W}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{X}{L}}}{\mathbf{W}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{X}{L}}}{\mathbf{W}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{X}{L}}}{\mathbf{W}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{X}{L}}}=\left|\frac{\partial{\delta x}_{\mathscr{E}}^{{\tilde{a}}}}{\partial{\Delta l}^{\mathscr{O}}_{{\tilde{b}}}}\right|\neq 0.

(68)

The physical importance of this condition is presented in the next sub–section. For now, let us consider it as a mathematical assumption.

Under the condition (
68
), and fixed initial conditions at the observer, one can locally represent the the generating function
S\left(\delta{x}^{\tilde{a}},\Delta{l}_{\tilde{b}};\lambda\right)
in terms of the boundary values of the canonical coordinates.

\displaystyle S\left({\delta x}_{\mathscr{E}}^{{\tilde{a}}},{\Delta l}^{\mathscr{E}}_{{\tilde{b}}};\lambda\right)\rightarrow S\left({\delta x}_{\mathscr{E}}^{{\tilde{a}}},{\delta x}_{\mathscr{O}}^{{\tilde{b}}};\lambda\right).

(69)

This is achieved by considering
{\Delta l}^{\mathscr{E}}_{{\tilde{b}}}={\Delta l}^{\mathscr{E}}_{{\tilde{b}}}\left({\delta x}_{\mathscr{O}}^{{\tilde{b}}}\right)
. Then, the generating function can be written as a quadratic form which follows as
[
73
]

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{\mathbf{\delta x}}{{}^{\intercal}_{\mathscr{O}}}\left(\mathchoice{\mathbf{W}^{{-1}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.50157pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.50157pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.08333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.91666pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{X}{L}}}{\mathbf{W}^{{-1}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.50157pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.50157pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.08333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.91666pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{X}{L}}}{\mathbf{W}^{{-1}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.50157pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.50157pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.08333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.91666pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{X}{L}}}{\mathbf{W}^{{-1}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.50157pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.50157pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.08333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.91666pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{X}{L}}}\mathchoice{\mathbf{W}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{X}{X}}}{\mathbf{W}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{X}{X}}}{\mathbf{W}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{X}{X}}}{\mathbf{W}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{X}{X}}}\right){\mathbf{\delta x}}{{}_{\mathscr{O}}}-{\mathbf{\delta x}}{{}^{\intercal}_{\mathscr{O}}}\left(\mathchoice{\mathbf{W}^{{-1}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.50157pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.50157pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.08333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.91666pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{X}{L}}}{\mathbf{W}^{{-1}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.50157pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.50157pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.08333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.91666pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{X}{L}}}{\mathbf{W}^{{-1}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.50157pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.50157pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.08333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.91666pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{X}{L}}}{\mathbf{W}^{{-1}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.50157pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.50157pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.08333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.91666pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{X}{L}}}\right){\mathbf{\delta x}}{{}_{\mathscr{E}}}

(70)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbf{\delta x}}{{}^{\intercal}_{\mathscr{E}}}\left(\mathchoice{\mathbf{W}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{L}{L}}}{\mathbf{W}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{L}{L}}}{\mathbf{W}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{L}{L}}}{\mathbf{W}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{L}{L}}}\mathchoice{\mathbf{W}^{{-1}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.50157pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.50157pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.08333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.91666pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{X}{L}}}{\mathbf{W}^{{-1}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.50157pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.50157pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.08333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.91666pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{X}{L}}}{\mathbf{W}^{{-1}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.50157pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.50157pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.08333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.91666pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{X}{L}}}{\mathbf{W}^{{-1}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.50157pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.50157pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.08333pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.91666pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{X}{L}}}\right){\mathbf{\delta x}}{{}_{\mathscr{E}}}.

Next, it can be shown that the Hamilton–Jacobi equation

\displaystyle\frac{dS}{d\lambda}=-\mathcal{H},

(71)

follows from eqs. (
II.4
) and (
70
). The generating function
S
satisfies

\displaystyle-{\Delta l}^{\mathscr{O}}_{{\tilde{b}}}=\frac{dS}{d\left({\delta x}_{\mathscr{O}}^{{\tilde{b}}}\right)},\,\,{\Delta l}^{\mathscr{E}}_{{\tilde{b}}}=\frac{dS}{d\left({\delta x}_{\mathscr{E}}^{{\tilde{b}}}\right)},

(72)

where
{\Delta l}^{\mathscr{O}}_{{\tilde{b}}}
and
{\Delta l}^{\mathscr{E}}_{{\tilde{b}}}
play the role of the canonical momenta at the boundary points. The reader might refer to the Appendix of
[
69
]
for explicit calculations to prove eq. (
71
) for the case of reduced Hamiltonian dynamics.

The quadratic generating function (
70
) can be put in a more compact form

\displaystyle S\left({\delta x}_{\mathscr{E}}^{{\tilde{a}}},{\delta x}_{\mathscr{O}}^{{\tilde{b}}};\lambda\right)=\frac{1}{2}\bm{X}^{\intercal}\mathbf{U}^{\intercal}\bm{X},\,\,{\rm{with}}\,\,\bm{X}=\begin{bmatrix}{\delta x}_{\mathscr{O}}^{{\tilde{b}}}\\
{\delta x}_{\mathscr{E}}^{{\tilde{b}}}\end{bmatrix}.

(73)

We write
\mathbf{U}
in the block form as

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}=\left[\begin{array}[]{c|c}\mathchoice{\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{O}\mathscr{O}}}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\tilde{a}}{\tilde{b}}}}{\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{O}\mathscr{O}}}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\tilde{a}}{\tilde{b}}}}{\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{O}\mathscr{O}}}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\tilde{a}}{\tilde{b}}}}{\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{O}\mathscr{O}}}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\tilde{a}}{\tilde{b}}}}&\mathchoice{\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{O}\mathscr{E}}}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\tilde{a}}{\tilde{b}}}}{\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{O}\mathscr{E}}}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\tilde{a}}{\tilde{b}}}}{\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{O}\mathscr{E}}}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\tilde{a}}{\tilde{b}}}}{\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{O}\mathscr{E}}}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\tilde{a}}{\tilde{b}}}}\\
\hline\cr\mathchoice{\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{E}\mathscr{O}}}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\tilde{a}}{\tilde{b}}}}{\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{E}\mathscr{O}}}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\tilde{a}}{\tilde{b}}}}{\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{E}\mathscr{O}}}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\tilde{a}}{\tilde{b}}}}{\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{E}\mathscr{O}}}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\tilde{a}}{\tilde{b}}}}&\mathchoice{\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{E}\mathscr{E}}}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\tilde{a}}{\tilde{b}}}}{\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{E}\mathscr{E}}}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\tilde{a}}{\tilde{b}}}}{\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{E}\mathscr{E}}}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\tilde{a}}{\tilde{b}}}}{\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{E}\mathscr{E}}}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.55557pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\tilde{a}}{\tilde{b}}}}\end{array}\right].

The components of the
8\times 8
matrix
\mathbf{U}
involves the sub–blocks of the matrix
\mathbf{W}
through

\displaystyle\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{O}\mathscr{O}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathbf{W}^{-1}_{XL}\mathbf{W}_{XX},

\displaystyle\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{O}\mathscr{E}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}^{-1}_{XL},

\displaystyle\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{E}\mathscr{O}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}_{LL}\mathbf{W}^{-1}_{XL}\mathbf{W}_{XX}-\mathbf{W}_{LX},

\displaystyle\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{E}\mathscr{E}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathbf{W}_{LL}\mathbf{W}^{-1}_{XL}.

(77)

What we are ultimately doing here is to switch from an initial value problem to a boundary value problem. Specifically, we consider the first row of the symplectic matrix in eq. (
36
) and re–write it for
{\Delta l}^{\mathscr{O}}_{{\tilde{b}}}
. Then, we substitute this term in the second row eq. (
36
) (See Appendix C of
[
59
]
for a similar calculation.). This is how we obtain the relations in eq. (
77
).

Essentially, the matrix
\mathbf{U}
transforms the boundary value of the canonical coordinates to the boundary value of the momenta, i.e.,

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{\Delta l}^{\mathscr{O}}_{{\tilde{a}}}\\
-{\Delta l}^{\mathscr{E}}_{{\tilde{a}}}\end{bmatrix}=\mathbf{U}\begin{bmatrix}{\delta x}_{\mathscr{O}}^{{\tilde{b}}}\\
{\delta x}_{\mathscr{E}}^{{\tilde{b}}}\end{bmatrix}.

(78)

This linear transformation defines a map that sends the direct sum of the tangent space of the emitter and the observer to the direct sum of their cotangent spaces. Namely,

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}:T_{\mathscr{O}}M\oplus T_{\mathscr{E}}M\rightarrow T^{*}_{\mathscr{O}}M\oplus T^{*}_{\mathscr{E}}M.

(79)

Previously, we mentioned that
\mathbf{W}
is a symplectic matrix and therefore its sub–blocks satisfy certain symplecticity conditions given in eqs. (
55
)-(
60
). Then, the sub–blocks of the
\mathbf{U}
matrix are not independent from each other either. In fact, it is easy to show that

\displaystyle\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{O}\mathscr{O}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{O}\mathscr{O}}}^{\intercal},\qquad{\rm{(due\,\,to\,\,eq.~(\ref{eq:symp_cond_4}))}}

(80)

\displaystyle\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{O}\mathscr{E}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{E}\mathscr{O}}}^{\intercal},\qquad{\rm{(due\,\,to\,\,eqs.~(\ref{eq:symp_cond_4})\,{\rm{and}}\,(\ref{eq:symp_cond_6}))}}

(81)

\displaystyle\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{E}\mathscr{E}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{E}\mathscr{E}}}^{\intercal}.\qquad{\rm{(due\,\,to\,\,eq.~(\ref{eq:symp_cond_2}))}}

(82)

It follows that
\mathbf{U}
is a symmetric matrix, i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}^{\intercal}=\mathbf{U}.

(83)

Note that the relation between
\mathbf{W}
and
\mathbf{U}

is an invertible operation on matrices. It is easy to check that it maps symplectic matrices to symmetric matrices and vice versa. Accordingly, one can also represent the sub–clocks of
\mathbf{W}
with respect to the sub–clocks of
\mathbf{U}
through

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}_{XX}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{O}\mathscr{E}}}^{-1}\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{O}\mathscr{O}}},

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}_{XL}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{O}\mathscr{E}}}^{-1},

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}_{LX}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{E}\mathscr{E}}}\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{O}\mathscr{E}}}^{-1}\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{O}\mathscr{O}}}-\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{E}\mathscr{O}}},

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}_{LL}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{E}\mathscr{E}}}\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{O}\mathscr{E}}}^{-1},

(84)

i.e., same set of relations between the sub–blocks of two matrices as in eq. (
77
).

Let us just briefly mention that both
\mathbf{W}
and
\mathbf{U}
need to satisfy additional algebraic relations associated with the null tangent vector
\vec{l}

[
52
,
59
]
. They will not play an important role in this work, apart from the calculations in Appendix
A
, so we will not refer to them here in detail.

With these results, the generating function (
70
) of our Hamiltonian formulation can simply be re–written as

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{1}{2}{\mathbf{\delta x}}{{}^{\intercal}_{\mathscr{O}}}\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{O}\mathscr{O}}}{\mathbf{\delta x}}{{}_{\mathscr{O}}}+{\mathbf{\delta x}}{{}^{\intercal}_{\mathscr{O}}}\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{O}\mathscr{E}}}{\mathbf{\delta x}}{{}_{\mathscr{E}}}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbf{\delta x}}{{}^{\intercal}_{\mathscr{E}}}\mathbf{U^{\mathscr{E}\mathscr{E}}}{\mathbf{\delta x}}{{}_{\mathscr{E}}}\right),

which can be viewed as the second order expansion of the world function,
\sigma
, of the null geodesic that connects the observer and the emitter.

II.5
Symmetries and reciprocity relations

Reciprocity relations in physics refer to the fact that certain measurement outcomes are unchanged under the change of input and output variables. An example for the case of relativistic observations is Etherington’s distance reciprocity
[
60
]
. This theorem states that the angular diameter distance is equal to the luminosity distance up to a redshift factor, i.e,

\displaystyle D^{\prime}_{L}=\left(1+z\right)\,D_{A}.

(86)

In addition, when the relationship between the relativistic, or corrected, luminosity distance,
D^{\prime}_{L}
, and the bolometric or uncorrected luminosity distance,
D_{L}
, is taken into account one obtains the distance duality relation,

\displaystyle D_{L}=\left(1+z\right)^{2}\,D_{A}.

(87)

In the literature, the proof of the distance reciprocity is shown for momentary observations where the observer is located at the vertex of a light bundle
[
74
]
. One defines a
2\times 2
Jacobi matrix,
\mathbf{D}
, that transforms the transverse components of the derivative of the geodesic deviation vector,
\delta\vec{x}
, along
\vec{l}
at the initial point to the transverse components of the geodesic deviation vector at the output point
3
3

3

Note that in
[
27
,
52
]
we denoted the Jacobi matrix with
\cal D
instead of
\mathbf{D}
. In the current paper, we reserve the symbol
\cal D
to express the Fermi–Walker derivative operator as in Section
IV.1
.
. In other words, it transforms the solid angle of the observer to the cross–sectional area calculated at the emitter. The determinant of the Jacobi matrix, thus gives the squared distances up to a frequency factor. The proof of reciprocity of distances follows from the fact that the value of the Jacobi matrix is equal to its minus transpose when the input and output variables are traversed. That is
\mathchoice{\mathbf{D}}{\mathbf{D}}{\mathbf{D}}{\mathbf{D}}\left(\lambda_{\mathscr{E}},\lambda_{\mathscr{O}}\right)=-\mathchoice{\mathbf{D}^{{\intercal}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.52779pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.52779pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.94444pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}{\mathbf{D}^{{\intercal}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.52779pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.52779pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.94444pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}{\mathbf{D}^{{\intercal}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.52779pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.52779pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.94444pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}{\mathbf{D}^{{\intercal}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.52779pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.52779pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.94444pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}\left(\lambda_{\mathscr{O}},\lambda_{\mathscr{E}}\right)
. Then, their determinants are equal to each other. Hence, the distance reciprocity holds, with the redshift pre–factor appearing in (
86
) due to the differences in the solid angles at the two end points, caused by the stellar aberration effect. It is also possible to explain this difference as resulting from the differences in infinitesimal proper distances,
d\ell=\omega d\lambda
. In
[
69
]
, it was shown that such a physical outcome follows from the symplectic symmetries of the geodesic deviation evolution defined on a reduced phase space. It was demonstrated that there exists a
4\times 4
matrix which consists of the
2\times 2
Jacobi matrix
\mathbf{D}
on its upper right block. It was argued that the distance reciprocity holds even for light propagation in multiple geometries due the symplectic symmetries. The underlying reason for such a symmetry essentially follows from the symmetries of the Riemann tensor
[
74
]
.

Here, we consider the generalization of the symplectic matrix presented in
[
69
]
. Compared to the one in
[
69
]
, the symplectic matrix,
\mathbf{W}
, considered in the current work involves two additional degrees of freedom. As we discussed in the previous section, there exists a symmetric matrix,
\mathbf{U}
, associated with
\mathbf{W}
. Those two matrices carry the same set of information. This is also relevant for the reciprocity relations. In Section
V
, we show that the symplectic symmetries of the underlying initial value problem, can be traded for the symmetries of the corresponding boundary value problem. Then, one can use the symmetry property of
\mathbf{U}
in order to prove the reciprocity relations.

In the current work, we assume that there exists no light caustics between the emitter and the observer. For this, one requires
\det\mathbf{D}\neq 0
such that the distances can be calculated
[
76
]
. In the following sections we pick such a parallel propagated tetrad that the
2\times 2
Jacobi matrix corresponds to the lower right block of our
4\times 4
matrix
\mathchoice{\mathbf{W}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{X}{L}}}{\mathbf{W}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{X}{L}}}{\mathbf{W}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{X}{L}}}{\mathbf{W}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{X}{L}}}
. Namely,

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathbf{W}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{X}{L}}}{\mathbf{W}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{X}{L}}}{\mathbf{W}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{X}{L}}}{\mathbf{W}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{X}{L}}}=\left[\begin{array}[]{c|c}\mathbf{A}&\,\mathbf{B}\\
\hline\cr\mathbf{C}&\,\mathbf{D}\end{array}\right]

For block matrices as above, if
\mathbf{D}
is invertible, then the determinant of the matrix is given by

\displaystyle\det\mathchoice{\mathbf{W}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{X}{L}}}{\mathbf{W}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{X}{L}}}{\mathbf{W}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{X}{L}}}{\mathbf{W}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{X}{L}}}={\rm{det}}\left(\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{B}\mathbf{D}^{-1}\mathbf{C}\right)\det\mathbf{D}.

(91)

For the matrix components calculated through our tetrad, it can be shown that
{\rm{det}}\left(\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{B}\mathbf{D}^{-1}\mathbf{C}\right)\neq 0
at each point of the propagation. Thus, assuming
\det\mathbf{D}\neq 0
guarantees that
\det\mathchoice{\mathbf{W}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{X}{L}}}{\mathbf{W}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{X}{L}}}{\mathbf{W}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{X}{L}}}{\mathbf{W}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.10211pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.44402pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.1743pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.48615pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{X}{L}}}\neq 0
holds, see Appedix
A
for the details of the proof. The physical interpretation of this result is the following:
\mathbf{W}_{XL}
is invertible if and only if the emitter is not situated on a caustic with respect to the observation point
p_{\mathscr{O}}
. In other words,
\mathbf{W}_{XL}
can only become non–invertible on the spatial transverse subspace, or the screen space. Emergence of a singular direction in the screen space is by definition a sign of a caustic.

Recall that in Section
II.4
, we argued that eq. (
68
) should hold in order to have an evolution problem represented by a free canonical transformation. Now it is clear that this condition is indeed equivalent to having a caustic–free assumption for the envelope of the family of light rays in question.

With this, we note that the aim of this paper is not only to outline the mathematical symmetries of variable observations, but also their physical outcomes. Switching from an initial value problem to a boundary value problem allows us to investigate the problem with matrices whose
symmetries are easier to handle. In addition, it allows us to find straightforward answers to questions regarding the physics of the problem. We are specifically interested in simultaneous variations that take place at the emitter’s and at the observer’s frame during the course of an observation that takes place within a finite interval of time. Switching to the boundary value problem allows us to asks questions such as “Given that the emitter and the observer have non–zero position variations how would that affect the observer’s recordings of the redshifts?”. In return, the symmetry of
\mathbf{U}
allows us to find an answer to such questions which we investigate in detail in Section
V
.

III
Scalar observables and bi–local variations

Let us now identify the relevance of matrix
\mathbf{U}
for generic observables. For this, we denote an operator
\bm{{U}}\left(\cdot,\cdot\right)
associated with the multiplication operator of the matrix
\mathbf{U}
. Its properties are as follows

(i)

\bm{{U}}\left(\bm{X},\cdot\right)
creates an 8–dimensional covector,

\displaystyle\left[\mathbf{U}\cdot\bm{X}\right]^{\intercal}:=\bm{\Delta L}^{\intercal}_{\bm{X}}=\left[{\Delta l}^{\mathscr{O}}_{{\tilde{a}}}\,\,\,\,\,-{\Delta l}^{\mathscr{E}}_{{\tilde{a}}}\right],

(92)

through eq. (
78
). Here,
\bm{X}
refers to a bi–local variation vector. The covector
\bm{\Delta L}^{\intercal}_{\bm{X}}
represents how the tangent vectors at
p_{\mathscr{O}}\,
and
p_{\mathscr{E}}\,
change given the possible variations of positions at the corresponding end points by
\bm{X}
. The bi–local variation vector might represent the variations at
\mathscr{O}\,
only, at
\mathscr{E}\,
only or both. We write it in general as

\displaystyle\bm{X}=\begin{bmatrix}{\delta x}_{\mathscr{O}}^{{\tilde{a}}}\\
{\delta x}_{\mathscr{E}}^{{\tilde{b}}}\end{bmatrix}.

(93)

Note that
\bm{\Delta L}_{\bm{X}}
also contains the information about how the deviation vectors at two points change along the null direction through the
Lie dragging
condition. Namely, through
\nabla_{\bm{X}}\vec{l}=\nabla_{\vec{l}}\bm{X}
. With this notation, we imply

\left(\nabla_{\delta\vec{x}}\vec{l}=\nabla_{\vec{l}}\,\delta\vec{x}\right)\Big|_{\mathscr{O}}
and
\left(\nabla_{\delta\vec{x}}\vec{l}=\nabla_{\vec{l}}\,\delta\vec{x}\right)\Big|_{\mathscr{E}}
, where
\delta\vec{x}
is a solution of the first order geodesic deviation eq. (
4
).

(ii)

\bm{{U}}\left(\bm{X},\bm{Y}\right)
creates a scalar,
\bm{X}^{\intercal}\mathbf{U}^{\intercal}\bm{Y}=\bm{\Delta L}^{\intercal}_{\bm{X}}\bm{Y}
, which is essentially the projection of
\bm{\Delta L}^{\intercal}_{\bm{X}}
on some arbitrary 8–dimensional vector
\bm{Y}
. This scalar has the potential of identifying the observables affected by the variations,
\bm{X}
, that can be recorded by
\mathscr{E}\,
, by
\mathscr{O}\,
or both depending on the vector
\bm{Y}
. For the time being, one might think of representing
\bm{Y}
through

\displaystyle\bm{Y}=\begin{bmatrix}{Y}^{\mathscr{O}}_{\tilde{a}}\\
{Y}^{\mathscr{E}}_{\tilde{b}}\end{bmatrix},

(94)

where
{Y}_{\mathscr{O}}^{\tilde{a}}
and
{Y}_{\mathscr{E}}^{\tilde{b}}
are components of some arbitrary projection vectors represented in a parallel propagated frame. Note that for all important observables we study in this paper,
\bm{Y}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbf{L}^{\intercal}\bm{Y}=0,\qquad{\rm{with}}\qquad\mathbf{L}^{\intercal}=\left[{l}^{\mathscr{O}}_{\mu^{\prime}}\,\,\,\,\,-{l}^{\mathscr{E}}_{\mu}\right].

(95)

In other words,
\bm{Y}
spans the tangent space to the local surface of communication mentioned before and described in
[
59
]
.

(iii)

\bm{{U}}
is a symmetric operator, i.e.,

\displaystyle\bm{{U}}\left(\bm{X},\bm{Y}\right)=\bm{{U}}\left(\bm{Y},\bm{X}\right).

(96)

This follows from

\displaystyle\bm{{U}}\left(\bm{X},\bm{Y}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bm{X}^{\intercal}\mathbf{U}^{\intercal}\bm{Y}=\left(\bm{X}^{\intercal}\mathbf{U}^{\intercal}\bm{Y}\right)^{\intercal}

(97)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bm{Y}^{\intercal}\mathbf{U}\bm{X}=\bm{Y}^{\intercal}\mathbf{U}^{\intercal}\bm{X}=\bm{{U}}\left(\bm{Y},\bm{X}\right),

which holds due to
\mathbf{U}
being a symmetric matrix.
This means the variations represented by
\bm{X}
and projected on
\bm{Y}
results in the same observables as variations represented by
\bm{Y}
and projected on
\bm{X}
. Then, in the case that both the variation vectors and the projection vectors are the solutions of the first order geodesic deviation equation for a null geodesic, the corresponding observables can be recorded covariantly during observations that allow both ends to be varied.

(iv)

\bm{{U}}\left(\bm{X},\bm{X}\right)
gives the generating function of the underlying dynamics through eq. (
II.4
). Its derivative along the propagation direction gives minus the Hamiltonian function of the system.

(v)

The symmetry property of
\bm{{U}}\left(\bm{X},\bm{Y}\right)
is unaffected even though one uses the coordinate components of bi–local vectors as inputs. For instance, we write

\displaystyle\bm{{U}}\left(\bm{X},\bm{Y}\right)=

\displaystyle\mathchoice{U^{{\mathscr{O}}{\mathscr{O}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\vec{{Y}_{\mathscr{O}}}}{\vec{{\delta x}_{\mathscr{O}}}}}}{U^{{\mathscr{O}}{\mathscr{O}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\vec{{Y}_{\mathscr{O}}}}{\vec{{\delta x}_{\mathscr{O}}}}}}{U^{{\mathscr{O}}{\mathscr{O}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\vec{{Y}_{\mathscr{O}}}}{\vec{{\delta x}_{\mathscr{O}}}}}}{U^{{\mathscr{O}}{\mathscr{O}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\vec{{Y}_{\mathscr{O}}}}{\vec{{\delta x}_{\mathscr{O}}}}}}+\mathchoice{U^{{\mathscr{O}}{\mathscr{E}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\vec{{Y}_{\mathscr{O}}}}{\vec{{\delta x}_{\mathscr{E}}}}}}{U^{{\mathscr{O}}{\mathscr{E}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\vec{{Y}_{\mathscr{O}}}}{\vec{{\delta x}_{\mathscr{E}}}}}}{U^{{\mathscr{O}}{\mathscr{E}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\vec{{Y}_{\mathscr{O}}}}{\vec{{\delta x}_{\mathscr{E}}}}}}{U^{{\mathscr{O}}{\mathscr{E}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\vec{{Y}_{\mathscr{O}}}}{\vec{{\delta x}_{\mathscr{E}}}}}}

(98)

\displaystyle+\mathchoice{U^{{\mathscr{E}}{\mathscr{O}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\vec{{Y}_{\mathscr{E}}}}{\vec{{\delta x}_{\mathscr{O}}}}}}{U^{{\mathscr{E}}{\mathscr{O}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\vec{{Y}_{\mathscr{E}}}}{\vec{{\delta x}_{\mathscr{O}}}}}}{U^{{\mathscr{E}}{\mathscr{O}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\vec{{Y}_{\mathscr{E}}}}{\vec{{\delta x}_{\mathscr{O}}}}}}{U^{{\mathscr{E}}{\mathscr{O}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\vec{{Y}_{\mathscr{E}}}}{\vec{{\delta x}_{\mathscr{O}}}}}}+\mathchoice{U^{{\mathscr{E}}{\mathscr{E}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\vec{{Y}_{\mathscr{E}}}}{\vec{{\delta x}_{\mathscr{E}}}}}}{U^{{\mathscr{E}}{\mathscr{E}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\vec{{Y}_{\mathscr{E}}}}{\vec{{\delta x}_{\mathscr{E}}}}}}{U^{{\mathscr{E}}{\mathscr{E}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\vec{{Y}_{\mathscr{E}}}}{\vec{{\delta x}_{\mathscr{E}}}}}}{U^{{\mathscr{E}}{\mathscr{E}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\vec{{Y}_{\mathscr{E}}}}{\vec{{\delta x}_{\mathscr{E}}}}}}.

Each term above refers to contraction of the submatrices of
\mathbf{U}
with the corresponding vectors. If we consider the first term, for example, we have

\displaystyle\mathchoice{U^{{\mathscr{O}}{\mathscr{O}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\vec{{\delta x}_{\mathscr{O}}}}{\vec{{Y}_{\mathscr{O}}}}}}{U^{{\mathscr{O}}{\mathscr{O}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\vec{{\delta x}_{\mathscr{O}}}}{\vec{{Y}_{\mathscr{O}}}}}}{U^{{\mathscr{O}}{\mathscr{O}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\vec{{\delta x}_{\mathscr{O}}}}{\vec{{Y}_{\mathscr{O}}}}}}{U^{{\mathscr{O}}{\mathscr{O}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[6.5382pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\vec{{\delta x}_{\mathscr{O}}}}{\vec{{Y}_{\mathscr{O}}}}}}={\delta x}_{\mathscr{O}}^{\mu^{\prime}}U^{\mathscr{O}\mathscr{O}}_{{\mu^{\prime}}{\nu^{\prime}}}{Y}_{\mathscr{O}}^{\nu^{\prime}}={\delta x}_{\mathscr{O}}^{\tilde{a}}\mathbf{U}^{\mathscr{O}\mathscr{O}}_{{\tilde{a}}{\tilde{b}}}{Y}_{\mathscr{O}}^{\tilde{b}}.

(99)

Thus, even though it is
\mathbf{U}^{\mathscr{O}\mathscr{O}}_{{\tilde{a}}{\tilde{b}}}
that is known to be a symmetric matrix, the resultant scalar is unaffected from the usage of coordinate components. Therefore, from now on, we use coordinate components of the bi–local vectors in the demonstration of symmetries in the rest of the paper.

III.1
Permissible bi–local variations

In order to identify the physical outcomes of the algebraic symmetries of observations, one should first make sure that the emitter and the observer can send each other signals under generic variations at the both ends. Accordingly, in this section, we identify under which conditions
\mathscr{E}\,
and
\mathscr{O}\,
can keep on communicating. For example, if there is a variation at the end point
p_{\mathscr{O}}\,
by an amount equal to the 4–velocity of the observer, what should be the variation at
p_{\mathscr{E}}\,
so that the communication channel is intact? What if we have a strictly spacelike variation at
p_{\mathscr{O}}\,
, what should the variation at the other end be for a signal to reach
\mathscr{O}\,
?

As we noted in the previous sections, the variations at the both ends are not completely arbitrary. Rather, there exists a criteria given as in eq. (
21
) that

\displaystyle\langle\,\mathchoice{\vec{l}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\mathscr{O}}}}{\vec{l}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\mathscr{O}}}}{\vec{l}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\mathscr{O}}}}{\vec{l}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\mathscr{O}}}},\delta\mathchoice{\vec{x}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\mathscr{O}}}}{\vec{x}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\mathscr{O}}}}{\vec{x}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\mathscr{O}}}}{\vec{x}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\mathscr{O}}}}\,\rangle-\langle\,\mathchoice{\vec{l}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\mathscr{E}}}}{\vec{l}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\mathscr{E}}}}{\vec{l}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\mathscr{E}}}}{\vec{l}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\mathscr{E}}}},\delta\mathchoice{\vec{x}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\mathscr{E}}}}{\vec{x}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\mathscr{E}}}}{\vec{x}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\mathscr{E}}}}{\vec{x}^{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.37502pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.33472pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.38194pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{\mathscr{E}}}}\,\rangle=0,

(100)

should hold. This guarantees that
\mathscr{E}\,
and
\mathscr{O}\,
have a
null
connecting geodesic throughout their evolution. We refer to any set of bi–local variation,
\bm{X}
,
that guarantees the criteria in eq. (
100
) a
permissible
bi–local variation. We re–write this criteria in a more compact form, throughout the paper as
\mathbf{L}^{\intercal}\bm{X}=0
.

We now introduce semi–orthonormal tetrads;
\vec{{e}}_{\mathscr{E}}:=\{\vec{{u}}^{\mathscr{E}},\vec{{l}}^{\mathscr{E}},\vec{{e}}^{{}_{\mathscr{E}}}_{2},\vec{{e}}^{{}_{\mathscr{E}}}_{3},\}
at point
p_{\mathscr{E}}\,
and
\vec{{e}}_{\mathscr{O}}:=\{\vec{{u}}^{\mathscr{O}},\vec{{l}}^{\mathscr{O}},\vec{{e}}^{{}_{\mathscr{O}}}_{2},\vec{{e}}^{{}_{\mathscr{O}}}_{3},\}
at point
p_{\mathscr{O}}\,
. Those satisfy

\displaystyle\langle\,\vec{{u}},\vec{l}\,\,\rangle

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle 0,\qquad\langle\,\vec{l},\vec{l}\,\,\rangle=0,\qquad\langle\,\vec{{u}},\vec{{u}}\,\,\rangle=-1,

\displaystyle\langle\,\vec{{u}},\vec{{e}}_{A}\,\,\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\langle\,\vec{l},\vec{{e}}_{A}\,\,\rangle=0,\,\,\,\,\,\,\langle\,\vec{{e}}_{A},\vec{{e}}_{B}\,\,\rangle=\mathchoice{\delta^{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{A}{B}}}{\delta^{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{A}{B}}}{\delta^{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{A}{B}}}{\delta^{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{{A}{B}}},

(101)

both for the emitter’s and the observer’s frame.
Note that the only assumption we make here is that the tangent vector of the fiducial null geodesic is parallel transported throughout the propagation, i.e., the value of
\vec{{l}}^{\mathscr{E}}
is equal to the one of
\vec{{l}}^{\mathscr{O}}
. However, we do not assume that the tetrad at
p_{\mathscr{O}}\,
is parallel transported to
p_{\mathscr{E}}\,
or vice versa. Rather, we consider the aforementioned tetrad
\vec{\tilde{e}}
as an auxiliary tetrad, which is parallel propagated along
\vec{l}
, i.e.,
\nabla_{\vec{l}}\,\vec{\tilde{e}}=0
.
We remind that this tetrad does not have to be adopted either to the emitter’s or to the observer’s frame. The idea is to use it as a tool to be able to compare vectors at different spacetime points. Such a tetrad was previously introduced in
[
52
]
and studied in full detail. It was later used in
[
77
]
in order to calculate the bi–local geodesic operators in static spherically symmetric spacetimes. Here, we refer to it as a means to understand the underlying symmetries of some scalar observables. We come back to this point in a while.

Let us now return to the permissible bi–local variations. Keep in mind that some arbitrary bi–local variation as in eq. (
93
) can be expanded with respect to the tetrad bases
\vec{{e}}_{\mathscr{O}}
and
\vec{{e}}_{\mathscr{E}}
at two points. For example,

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}=\begin{bmatrix}{\left(\delta x\right)}^{u^{\prime}}{u}_{\mathscr{O}}^{\mu^{\prime}}+\left(\delta x\right)^{l^{\prime}}{l}_{\mathscr{O}}^{\mu^{\prime}}+\left(\delta x\right)^{A^{\prime}}{e}{{}^{\mu^{\prime}}_{\mathscr{O}A^{\prime}}}\\
{\left(\delta x\right)}^{u}{u}_{\mathscr{E}}^{\mu}+\left(\delta x\right)^{l}{l}_{\mathscr{E}}^{\mu}+\left(\delta x\right)^{A}{e}{{}^{\mu}_{\mathscr{E}A}}\end{bmatrix}.

(102)

Therefore, we first identify all possible permissible bi–local variations of the tetrad basis of the observer and the emitter. Those are

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bm{T}&=\begin{bmatrix}{u}_{\mathscr{O}}^{\mu^{\prime}}\\
\left(1+z\right)^{-1}{u}_{\mathscr{E}}^{\mu}\end{bmatrix},\,\,\,\,\,\bm{K_{\mathscr{O}}}=\begin{bmatrix}{l}_{\mathscr{O}}^{\mu^{\prime}}\\
0\end{bmatrix},\,\,\,\,\,\bm{K_{\mathscr{E}}}=\begin{bmatrix}0\\
{l}_{\mathscr{E}}^{\mu}\end{bmatrix},\end{split}

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bm{E}_{A^{\prime}}^{\mathscr{O}}&=\begin{bmatrix}{e}{{}^{\mu^{\prime}}_{\mathscr{O}A^{\prime}}}\\
0\end{bmatrix},\,\,\,\,\,\bm{E}_{A}^{\mathscr{E}}=\begin{bmatrix}0\\
{e}{{}^{\mu}_{\mathscr{E}B}}\end{bmatrix}.\end{split}

(103)

Those 7 bi–local vectors form a basis of the permissible variations space.
Here, z represents the redshift which is obtained through the ratio of the emitted frequency of light to its observed value, i.e.,

z=\frac{\langle\vec{l}_{\mathscr{E}},\vec{u}_{\mathscr{E}}\rangle}{\langle\vec{l}_{\mathscr{O}},\vec{u}_{\mathscr{O}}\rangle}-1.

(104)

It is straightforward to verify that all of the bi–local vectors in eq. (
103
) satisfy the permissibility criteria (
100
). This is due to the semi–orthonormal tetrad satisfying the properties presented in eq. (
101
) at
p_{\mathscr{E}}\,
and at
p_{\mathscr{O}}\,
.

Now that all possible permissible bilocal tetrad variations are obtained, one can consider possible observables corresponding to them. We mentioned in sub–section
III
that one can compose scalar observables through the operator
\bm{{U}}\left(\cdot,\cdot\right)
. Let us remind that this operator inputs two arbitrary bi–local variations,
\bm{X}
and
\bm{Y}
, that are given in the form in eq. (
102
). This means
\bm{X}
and
\bm{Y}
can be expanded with respect to the permissible bi–local tetrad variations listed in eq. (
103
).

For instance, let us consider a case where
\bm{X}=m\bm{E}_{A^{\prime}}^{\mathscr{O}}
with
{m}
being an arbitrary function and
\bm{Y}=\bm{T}
. Then,
\bm{{U}}\left({m\bm{E}_{A^{\prime}}^{\mathscr{O}}},{\bm{T}}\right)
inputs
m\bm{E}_{A^{\prime}}^{\mathscr{O}}
with the variation at the observer being
\delta\vec{x}^{\mathscr{O}}=m\vec{e}^{\mathscr{O}}_{A^{\prime}}
and the variation at emitter being
\delta\vec{x}^{\mathscr{E}}=\vec{0}
. It inputs
\bm{T}
with the projection at the observer being
\vec{Y}_{\mathscr{O}}=\vec{u}_{\mathscr{O}}
and the projection at emitter being
\vec{Y}_{\mathscr{E}}=\left(1+z\right)^{-1}\vec{u}_{\mathscr{E}}
. The result follows as

\displaystyle\bm{{U}}\left({m\bm{E}_{A^{\prime}}^{\mathscr{O}}},{\bm{T}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m\mathchoice{U^{{\mathscr{O}}{\mathscr{O}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.8493pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.8493pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.76042pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.76042pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.17569pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.17569pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.12569pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[11.12569pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\vec{u}_{\mathscr{O}}}{\vec{e}^{\mathscr{O}}_{{A^{\prime}}}}}}{U^{{\mathscr{O}}{\mathscr{O}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.8493pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.8493pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.76042pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.76042pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.17569pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.17569pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.12569pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[11.12569pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}_{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.13892pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.81111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\vec{u}_{\mathscr{O}}}{\vec{e}^{\mathscr{O}}_{{A^{\prime}}}}}}{U^{{\mathscr{O}}{\mathscr{O}}\mathchoice{\makebox[10.8493pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.8493pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.76042pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.76042pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.17569pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.17569pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.12569pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[11.12569pt][c]{$\scriptscriptst This gives us an observable associated with the variations at the end points
p_{\mathscr{O}}\,
and
p_{\mathscr{E}}\,
by an amount equal to
m\vec{e}^{\,\mathscr{O}}_{{A}^{\prime}}
and
\vec{0}
respectively, which is then measured by an observer with 4–velocity
\vec{u}_{\mathscr{O}}
and an emitter with 4–velocity
\vec{u}_{\mathscr{E}}
.

In the next section, we construct physical observables via such combinations of
\bm{{U}}\left(\bm{X},\bm{Y}\right)
. We then investigate the underlying symmetries of given observables in Section
V
by making use of the symmetries of the matrix
\mathbf{U}
.

IV
The observables on the sky

IV.1
Momentary observables

In this section, we recall the momentary optical observables, related to the measurement of light emitted by distant sources in a curved spacetime. Those observables are recorded via instantaneous measurements. We simply list them as

(i)

Redshift, which is defined through the ratio of emitted value of the frequency of light to the observed value. Here, for the sake of simplifying the expressions, we consider a logarithmic redshift

\displaystyle\ln\left(1+z\right)=\ln\left(\frac{\langle\,\vec{l}_{\mathscr{E}},\vec{u}_{\mathscr{E}}\,\rangle}{\langle\,\vec{l}_{\mathscr{O}},\vec{u}_{\mathscr{O}}\,\rangle}\right).

(106)

(ii)

Position vector, which is defined by the observer. It can be written with respect to his/her 4–velocity and the null propagation vector as

\displaystyle\vec{r}_{\mathscr{O}}=\vec{u}_{\mathscr{O}}+\omega_{\mathscr{O}}^{-1}\vec{l}_{\mathscr{O}},

(107)

with
\omega_{\mathscr{O}}=\langle\,{\vec{l}_{\mathscr{O}},\vec{u}_{\mathscr{O}}}\,\rangle
.
This vector satisfies

\displaystyle\langle\,\vec{r}_{\mathscr{O}},\vec{r}_{\mathscr{O}}\,\rangle=1,\qquad\langle\,\vec{r}_{\mathscr{O}},\vec{u}_{\mathscr{O}}\,\rangle=0,

(108)

i.e., it is normalized and spatial as viewed in the
observer’s
frame and it points in the direction from which the observer sees the light incoming.

(iii)

Viewing direction vector, which is defined by the emitter. It can be written with respect to his/her 4–velocity and the null propagation vector as

\displaystyle\vec{r}_{\mathscr{E}}=\vec{u}_{\mathscr{E}}+\omega_{\mathscr{E}}^{-1}\vec{l}_{\mathscr{E}},

(109)

with
\omega_{\mathscr{E}}=\langle\,{\vec{l}_{\mathscr{E}},\vec{u}_{\mathscr{E}}}\,\rangle
.
Similar to the case of the observer’s position vector, this vector satisfies

\displaystyle\langle\,\vec{r}_{\mathscr{E}},\vec{r}_{\mathscr{E}}\,\rangle=1,\qquad\langle\,\vec{r}_{\mathscr{E}},\vec{u}_{\mathscr{E}}\,\rangle=0.

(110)

A more operational definition of this quantity would be as follows: it defines the direction from which we are observing the source. If the emission is not perfectly isotropic, the results obtained by the observer will depend on
\vec{r}_{\mathscr{E}}
. The viewing direction is a normalized spatial vector in the
emitter’s
frame.

IV.2
Variational observables

Now, we consider those observables which can not be recorded via momentary observations. We classify those observables in two main branches: (i) Drift effects, which require timelike variation of the observer and the emitter. The recordings of the observables are taken as the observer propagates on his/her own timelike worldline. (ii) Effects of spatial variations, which are caused by the end point variation of the observer and the emitter in transverse directions. The recordings of the observables are taken as the observer and the emitter perform spatial variations. We should add that one is free to add a constant variation along
\vec{l}
to arbitrary transverse variations of the emitter and the observer. The corresponding effect is just a reparametrization of the null geodesic without its alteration and thus it is not physically measurable in any case. Also, note that displacing an observer or a single point light source along a spacelike vector is obviously nonphysical. However, in practice, these quantities can be measured indirectly. For example, one can obtain them by comparing the measurement recordings of two nearby observers, or by observing an extended source of known shape and size.

In this section, we see that the variational observables can be obtained through the aforementioned symmetric operator
\bm{{U}}\left(\cdot,\cdot\right)
associated with the matrix
\mathbf{U}
. Later, we analyse the relationship between those observables and investigate some relations similar to reciprocity relations via the symmetry properties of
\bm{{U}}\left(\cdot,\cdot\right)
.

IV.2.1
Drift effects

Imagine a signal being sent by an emitter continuously in between the time interval
\tau=\tau_{0}
and
\tau=\tau_{0}+\Delta\tau
where
\tau
is the emitter’s proper time. Those signals will be continuously received by an observer in the time interval
\tau^{\prime}=\tau^{\prime}_{0}
and
\tau^{\prime}=\tau^{\prime}_{0}+\Delta\tau^{\prime}
where
\tau^{\prime}
